Presidential elections were held in [[Portugal]] in 2026, with a first round on 18 January and a second round on 8 February.<ref>{{Cite web |date=14 September 2025 |title=Eleições presidenciais deverão ser marcadas para 18 de Janeiro de 2026 |url=https://www.publico.pt/2025/09/14/politica/noticia/eleicoes-presidenciais-deverao-marcadas-18-janeiro-2026-2147140 |access-date=14 September 2025 |publisher=Público |archive-date=30 January 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260130085946/https://www.publico.pt/2025/09/14/politica/noticia/eleicoes-presidenciais-deverao-marcadas-18-janeiro-2026-2147140 |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=30 October 2025 |title=Presidential Elections are scheduled |url=http://www.theportugalnews.com/news/2025-10-30/presidential-elections-are-scheduled/909563 |access-date=8 November 2025 |website=The Portugal News |language=en |archive-date=6 February 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260206143701/https://www.theportugalnews.com/news/2025-10-30/presidential-elections-are-scheduled/909563 |url-status=live }}</ref> The incumbent president, [[Marcelo Rebelo de Sousa]], supported by the [[Social Democratic Party (Portugal)|Social Democratic Party]] (PSD), had already served two consecutive terms, so he was not eligible for re-election.

Fourteen potential candidates submitted formal applications, of which eleven were certified to appear on the ballot paper.<ref name=":candidatos" /><ref name=":rejeitado" /> They included the former coordinator of [[COVID-19 vaccination in Portugal|Portugal's COVID-19 vaccination]] task force [[Henrique Gouveia e Melo]],<ref name=":7">{{Cite web |date=29 May 2025 |title=Gouveia e Melo lança candidatura à Presidência da República |url=https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/politica/gouveia-e-melo-lanca-candidatura-a-presidencia-da-republica_n1658527 |access-date=29 May 2025 |website=RTP |language=pt |archive-date=30 January 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260130172618/https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/politica/gouveia-e-melo-lanca-candidatura-a-presidencia-da-republica_n1658527 |url-status=live }}</ref> who ran as an [[independent politician|independent]], and former PSD leader [[Luís Marques Mendes]].<ref name=":8">{{Cite web |date=6 February 2025 |title=Marques Mendes apresenta candidatura às eleições presidenciais |url=https://sicnoticias.pt/especiais/eleicoes-presidenciais/2025-02-06-video-marques-mendes-apresenta-candidatura-as-eleicoes-presidenciais-ef3e8787 |access-date=6 February 2025 |website=SIC Notícias |language=pt |archive-date=7 February 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260207182016/https://sicnoticias.pt/especiais/eleicoes-presidenciais/2025-02-06-video-marques-mendes-apresenta-candidatura-as-eleicoes-presidenciais-ef3e8787 |url-status=live }}</ref> The [[Socialist Party (Portugal)|Socialist Party]] (PS) supported the campaign of their former party leader [[António José Seguro]].<ref>{{Cite web |title="Se Augusto Santos Silva não for candidato não vejo como será possível o PS não apoiar António José Seguro", diz Duarte Cordeiro |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=17 September 2025 |title=Decisão do PS de apoio a Seguro já tem data: 19 de outubro (uma semana depois das autárquicas) |url=https://expresso.pt/politica/eleicoes/presidenciais/2025-09-17-decisao-do-ps-de-apoio-a-seguro-ja-tem-data-19-de-outubro--uma-semana-depois-das-autarquicas--8b6f5e0e |access-date=19 September |website=SIC Notícias |language=pt |archive-date=3 July 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250703083559/https://sicnoticias.pt/especiais/eleicoes-presidenciais/2025-07-02-presidenciais-carneiro-insiste-que-eventual-apoio-do-ps-so-sera-decidido-apos-autarquicas-ee364587 |url-status=live }}</ref> [[André Ventura]], the leader of [[Chega]] (CH), also stood.<ref name=":chega">{{Cite web |date=16 September 2025 |title=Presidenciais. André Ventura avança com candidatura a Belém |url=https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/pais/presidenciais-andre-ventura-avanca-com-candidatura-a-belem_n1684059 |access-date=16 September 2025 |website=RTP |language=pt |archive-date=17 October 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20251017135259/https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/pais/presidenciais-andre-ventura-avanca-com-candidatura-a-belem_n1684059 |url-status=live }}</ref> Other candidates supported by parties were the MEPs [[João Cotrim de Figueiredo]] for [[Liberal Initiative]] (IL) and [[Catarina Martins]] for [[Left Bloc]] (BE); the former MP [[António Filipe (politician)|António Filipe]] for the [[Portuguese Communist Party]] (PCP); and the MP [[Jorge Pinto]] for [[LIVRE]] (L).

In the first round, Seguro (PS) won the most votes with 31%, while Ventura (CH) came second with 23.5%.<ref>{{Cite news |date=18 January 2026 |title=Socialist, far-right candidate head for Portugal's presidential runoff |url=https://www.reuters.com/world/europe/portugal-votes-tight-presidential-race-with-far-right-poised-reach-runoff-2026-01-18/ |access-date=18 January 2026 |work=[[Reuters]]}}</ref> Because no candidate reached the required 50% threshold, Seguro and Ventura faced each other in a second round run-off on 8 February.<ref>{{Cite web |date=18 January 2026 |title=Center left wins Portugal presidential election first round, exit poll says |url=https://www.politico.eu/article/far-right-wins-portugal-presidential-election-first-round-exit-poll-says/ |access-date=18 January 2026 |website=[[Politico]] |archive-date=20 January 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260120152608/https://www.politico.eu/article/far-right-wins-portugal-presidential-election-first-round-exit-poll-says/ |url-status=live }}</ref> This was only the second time that a direct Portuguese presidential election went to a second round, after [[1986 Portuguese presidential election|the 1986 election]].<ref>{{Cite news |date=18 January 2026 |title=Portugal votes in tight presidential race with far right poised to reach runoff |url=https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/jan/18/portugal-election-votes-tight-presidential-race-far-right-runoff |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260119014846/https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/jan/18/portugal-election-votes-tight-presidential-race-far-right-runoff |archive-date=19 January 2026 |access-date=19 January 2026 |work=[[The Guardian]] |language=en-GB |issn=0261-3077 |agency=[[Reuters]]}}</ref>

Candidates eliminated in the first round included Cotrim de Figueiredo (IL) who came third with 16%, and Gouveia e Melo (independent) fourth with 12%. Marques Mendes (PSD) received 11%, the lowest in Portuguese history for a government supported presidential candidate, surpassing the previous negative record set by [[Mário Soares]] in [[2006 Portuguese presidential election|2006]].<ref>{{Cite news |date=18 January 2026 |title=Mendes teve tantos votos da AD como Cotrim – e acaba com o pior resultado em Presidenciais de um candidato apoiado pelo Governo |url=https://expresso.pt/politica/eleicoes/presidenciais-2026/2026-01-18-mendes-teve-tantos-votos-da-ad-como-cotrim---e-acaba-com-o-pior-resultado-em-presidenciais-de-um-candidato-apoiado-pelo-governo-9bb46101 |access-date=19 January 2026 |website=Expresso |archive-date=19 January 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260119013714/https://expresso.pt/politica/eleicoes/presidenciais-2026/2026-01-18-mendes-teve-tantos-votos-da-ad-como-cotrim---e-acaba-com-o-pior-resultado-em-presidenciais-de-um-candidato-apoiado-pelo-governo-9bb46101 |url-status=live }}</ref> Catarina Martins (BE) received 2%, the lowest for a female candidate in a presidential election,<ref>{{Cite news |date=19 January 2026 |title=Catarina Martins foi a quinta mulher candidata e a que teve mais baixa votação |url=https://observador.pt/2026/01/19/catarina-martins-foi-a-quinta-mulher-candidata-e-a-que-teve-mais-baixa-votacao/ |access-date=19 January 2026 |website=Observador |archive-date=19 January 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260119011952/https://observador.pt/2026/01/19/catarina-martins-foi-a-quinta-mulher-candidata-e-a-que-teve-mais-baixa-votacao/ |url-status=live }}</ref> while António Filipe (PCP) received less than 2%, the Communists' worst result in a presidential election.<ref>{{Cite news |date=19 January 2026 |title=António Filipe com pior resultado de um candidato apoiado pelo PCP nas presidenciais |url=https://www.cmjornal.pt/cmtv/videos/detalhe/20260119-104500-antonio-filipe-com-pior-resultado-de-um-candidato-apoiado-pelo-pcp-nas-presidenciais |access-date=19 January 2026 |website=Correio da Manhã}}</ref>

Overall voter turnout (including overseas) in the first round was 52 percent, thirteen [[percentage point]]s higher than the previous election.<ref name="turnout">{{cite web |date=18 January 2026 |title=Presidenciais: Seguro e Ventura vão disputar a segunda volta |url=https://sapo.pt/artigo/presidenciais-seguro-e-ventura-vao-disputar-a-segunda-volta-696d67df28022cce3a68b39f |publisher=Sapo/A Bola}}</ref> In Portugal itself, turnout was 61.50 percent, an increase of 16.1 percentage points compared to 2021,<ref name="results">{{cite news |date=18 January 2026 |title=Presidenciais 2026 – Resultados |url=https://www.eleicoes.mai.gov.pt/presidenciais2026_1S/resultados/globais |access-date=5 February 2025 |publisher=mai.gov.pt |language=pt |archive-date=8 February 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260208212359/https://www.eleicoes.mai.gov.pt/presidenciais2026_1S/resultados/globais |url-status=live }}</ref> and the highest [[2006 Portuguese presidential election|since 2006]].<ref name=turnout /> Seguro defeated Ventura by a wide margin on the second round, 67% to 33%,<ref>{{Cite news |date=9 February 2026 |title=Center left beats far right to Portugal's presidency |url=https://www.politico.eu/article/exit-poll-center-left-wins-portuguese-presidency-antonio-seguro/ |access-date=8 February 2026 |work=Politico EU}}</ref> and became the most voted candidate ever in a Portuguese election by surpassing Soares' [[1991 Portuguese presidential election|1991]] vote total record.<ref>{{Cite news |date=8 February 2026 |title=Histórico: Seguro ultrapassa Soares e é o Presidente mais votado de sempre |url=https://sicnoticias.pt/especiais/eleicoes-presidenciais/2026/2026-02-08-historico-seguro-ultrapassa-soares-e-e-o-presidente-mais-votado-de-sempre-d551a604 |access-date=9 February 2026 |website=SIC Notícias}} {{Dead link|date=October 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }}</ref> Overall turnout in the second round dropped slightly to 50%. António José Seguro was sworn in as President on 9 March 2026.<ref>{{cite news |url=https://sicnoticias.pt/pais/politica/2026-03-09-video-tomada-de-posse-do-novo-presidente-momento-do-juramento-de-antonio-jose-seguro-5076a71b |title=Tomada de posse do novo Presidente: momento do juramento de António José Seguro |language=pt |work=SIC Notícias |access-date=9 March 2026 |date=9 March 2026}} {{Dead link|date=October 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }}</ref>

==Background==
[[Marcelo Rebelo de Sousa]] was re-elected in January 2021, securing nearly 61 percent of the votes in the first round. He took the oath of office on 9 March 2021, and continued the period of [[Cohabitation (government)|cohabitation]] with [[Socialist Party (Portugal)|Socialist Party]] Prime Minister [[António Costa]], which lasted until April 2024. This cohabitation ended after the [[2024 Portuguese legislative election|March 2024 election]], which saw [[Luís Montenegro]], from the [[Social Democratic Party (Portugal)|Social Democratic Party]] (the same party as the President), nominated as prime minister. During his second term, Marcelo Rebelo de Sousa suffered [[Opinion polling on the Marcelo Rebelo de Sousa presidency#Second Term (2021–present)|a considerable decline in his public approval]] according to polling,<ref>{{Cite web |title=Marcelo: de “absolutamente consensual” a uma “impossível” recuperação de popularidade |url=https://www.publico.pt/2024/05/04/politica/noticia/marcelo-absolutamente-consensual-impossivel-recuperacao-popularidade-2089159 |access-date=27 August 2026 |website=Público |language=pt |date=4 May 2024 }}</ref> following a series of controversies, such as gaffes surrounding abuses in the Catholic Church or regarding his opinions on prime ministers,<ref>{{Cite web |title=Abusos na Igreja e o recuo em três atos de Marcelo (sem pedir perdão): "infelizmente terá havido números muito superiores em Portugal" |url=https://expresso.pt/politica/2022-10-11-Abusos-na-Igreja-e-o-recuo-em-tres-atos-de-Marcelo--sem-pedir-perdao--infelizmente-tera-havido-numeros-muito-superiores-em-Portugal-b009a80c |access-date=27 August 2026 |website=Expresso |language=pt |date=11 October 2022 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |title=Marcelo diz que Montenegro “dá muito trabalho” e tem “comportamentos rurais” |url=https://www.publico.pt/2024/04/24/politica/noticia/marcelo-montenegro-trabalho-comportamentos-rurais-2088190 |access-date=27 August 2026 |website=Público |language=pt |date=24 April 2024 }}</ref> a case involving his son, and splashing the president, related to an alleged irregular and privileged access to an expensive healthcare treatment for twins of [[Brazilians|Brazilian]] origin,<ref>{{Cite web |title=Caso das gémeas. A "situação desagradável" que abalou o Presidente Marcelo e o levou a "cortar" relações com o filho  |url=https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/pais/caso-das-gemeas-a-situacao-desagradavel-que-abalou-o-presidente-marcelo-e-o-levou-a-cortar-relacoes-com-o-filho_n1723088 |access-date=27 August 2026 |website=RTP |language=pt |date=4 March 2026 }}</ref> and the succession of parliamentary dissolutions, three in just under 4 years,<ref>{{Cite web |title=Dez anos, três crises políticas. As dissoluções do presidente Marcelo |url=https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/pais/dez-anos-tres-crises-politicas-as-dissolucoes-do-presidente-marcelo_n1722836 |access-date=27 August 2026 |website=RTP |language=pt |date=3 March 2026 }}</ref> the most contentious being the 2024 one, after Costa's resignation following [[Operation Influencer]], and with the president rejecting alternative solutions proposed by the ruling Socialist Party.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Dissolução do Parlamento é oficial: decreto do Presidente foi publicado |url=https://expresso.pt/presidencia-republica/2024-01-15-Dissolucao-do-Parlamento-e-oficial-decreto-do-Presidente-foi-publicado-e577f98e |access-date=27 August 2026 |website=Expresso |language=pt |date=15 January 2024 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |title=Marcelo recusou Centeno para substituir Costa e marcou eleições para 10 de Março |url=https://www.publico.pt/2023/11/09/politica/noticia/detidos-operacao-influencer-levou-demissao-costa-comecam-ouvidos-2069582 |access-date=27 August 2026 |website=Público |language=pt |date=9 November 2023 }}</ref> The relationship between Marcelo and the two prime ministers during his second term was also tense, ranging from disagreements with Costa over controversies in his government,<ref>{{Cite web |title=Após choque frontal com Costa, Marcelo vai falar ao país às 20 horas |url=https://eco.sapo.pt/2023/05/04/apos-choque-frontal-com-costa-marcelo-vai-falar-ao-pais-as-20-horas/ |access-date=27 August 2026 |website=ECO |language=pt |date=4 May 2023 }}</ref> to a distant and cold one with Montenegro.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Marcelo e Montenegro: entre a coabitação distante e os desencontros  |url=https://www.publico.pt/2025/03/08/politica/noticia/marcelo-montenegro-coabitacao-distante-desencontros-2125218 |access-date=27 August 2026 |website=Público |language=pt |date=8 March 2025 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |title=Relação distante q.b. entre Marcelo e Montenegro: os sinais de desagrado do Presidente |url=https://expresso.pt/presidencia-republica/2024-09-11-relacao-distante-q.b.-entre-marcelo-e-montenegro-os-sinais-de-desagrado-do-presidente-2ae45c1a |access-date=27 August 2026 |website=Expresso |language=pt |date=11 September 2024 }}</ref>

Within the Portuguese political system, the president serves as the [[head of state]] with primarily ceremonial duties, though the president holds some political influence and can dissolve Parliament during a crisis. The president resides at the [[Belém Palace]] in Lisbon. Since the Carnation Revolution, all Portuguese presidents have been re-elected for a second term and never tried a third, with one exception: [[Mário Soares]] (PS), who sought a non-consecutive third term in the [[2006 Portuguese presidential election|2006 presidential election]] but lost. Thus, every president since 1976 has served exactly two terms.

===Pre-campaign===
Admiral [[Henrique Gouveia e Melo]], who rose to prominence as the coordinator of Portugal's [[COVID-19 Vaccination Plan Task Force (Portugal)|COVID-19 Vaccination Task Force]],<ref>{{Cite web |title=Gouveia e Melo agradece à população e aos profissionais de saúde no adeus |url=https://cnnportugal.iol.pt/covid-19/coronavirus/gouveia-e-melo-agradece-a-populacao-e-aos-profissionais-de-saude-no-adeus |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=CNN Portugal |language=pt |archive-date=18 January 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260118112445/https://cnnportugal.iol.pt/covid-19/coronavirus/gouveia-e-melo-agradece-a-populacao-e-aos-profissionais-de-saude-no-adeus |url-status=live }}</ref> quickly emerged as a leading candidate in early polling as far back as mid-2022.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Sondagem TVI/CNN: Gouveia e Melo, Costa e Passos Coelho são os candidatos presidenciais com maior potencial. Sócrates e Jerónimo no fim da tabela |url=https://cnnportugal.iol.pt/presidenciais/candidatos/sondagem-tvi-cnn-gouveia-e-melo-costa-e-passos-coelho-sao-os-candidatos-presidenciais-com-maior-potencial-socrates-e-jeronimo-no-fim-da-tabela/20220718/62d54d950cf2ea4f0a546db7 |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=CNN Portugal |language=pt |archive-date=15 September 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250915094102/https://cnnportugal.iol.pt/presidenciais/candidatos/sondagem-tvi-cnn-gouveia-e-melo-costa-e-passos-coelho-sao-os-candidatos-presidenciais-com-maior-potencial-socrates-e-jeronimo-no-fim-da-tabela/20220718/62d54d950cf2ea4f0a546db7 |url-status=live }}</ref> Over the next two years, he repeatedly oscillated in his public statements regarding his intentions to run, fueling speculation.<ref>{{Cite web |date=28 October 2021 |title=Gouveia e Melo volta a afastar entrada na política: "Se isso acontecer, dêem-me uma corda para me enforcar" |url=https://www.publico.pt/2021/10/28/sociedade/noticia/gouveia-melo-volta-afastar-entrada-politica-acontecer-deemme-corda-enforcar-1982887 |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=PÚBLICO |language=pt |agency=Lusa |archive-date=20 November 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20251120005034/https://www.publico.pt/2021/10/28/sociedade/noticia/gouveia-melo-volta-afastar-entrada-politica-acontecer-deemme-corda-enforcar-1982887 |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=11 March 2023 |title=Gouveia e Melo sobre candidatura à Presidência da República: "A pergunta é legítima" |url=https://sicnoticias.pt/pais/2023-03-11-Gouveia-e-Melo-sobre-candidatura-a-Presidencia-da-Republica-A-pergunta-e-legitima-3c6583ff |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=SIC Notícias |language=pt}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=18 May 2023 |title="Quando eu digo não é não." Gouveia e Melo afasta candidatura à presidência – Renascença |url=https://rr.pt/noticia/politica/2023/05/18/quando-eu-digo-nao-e-nao-gouveia-e-melo-afasta-candidatura-a-presidencia/331902/ |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=Rádio Renascença |language=pt-pt}}</ref> By November 2024, having declined to be renominated as [[Chief of the Naval Staff (Portugal)|Chief of the Naval Staff]], Gouveia e Melo started preparing his path for the presidency, signaling the support from local politicians<ref>{{Cite web |date=23 November 2024 |title=Gouveia e Melo deverá anunciar candidatura presidencial em março |url=https://sicnoticias.pt/pais/2024-11-23-gouveia-e-melo-devera-anunciar-candidatura-presidencial-em-marco-a741a6ac |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=SIC Notícias |language=pt |archive-date=23 April 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250423155503/https://sicnoticias.pt/pais/2024-11-23-gouveia-e-melo-devera-anunciar-candidatura-presidencial-em-marco-a741a6ac |url-status=live }}</ref> and stating that he didn't want the support of any party.<ref>{{Cite web |date=28 November 2024 |title=Gouveia e Melo quer manter-se livre de partidos |url=https://expresso.pt/politica/2024-11-28-gouveia-e-melo-quer-manter-se-livre-de-partidos-4b73d90a |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=Expresso |language=pt |archive-date=26 March 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250326203921/https://expresso.pt/politica/2024-11-28-gouveia-e-melo-quer-manter-se-livre-de-partidos-4b73d90a |url-status=live }}</ref> He ultimately announced his candidacy in May 2025.<ref name=":7" />

[[File:Mendes avisa que cargo exige experiência política e não é tempo de "tiros no escuro" - YouTube - 0-2-16.jpg|thumb|right|Luís Marques Mendes and his wife at his campaign launch, 11 February 2025.]]
On the traditional centre-right, the [[Social Democratic Party (Portugal)|Social Democratic Party]] had several high-profile potential candidates for the presidency, including the former prime ministers [[Pedro Passos Coelho]] and [[Pedro Santana Lopes]],<ref>{{Cite web |date=30 November 2024 |title=Presidenciais 2026: "Se o Pedro Passos Coelho não for, eu sou capaz de ganhar ao Almirante Gouveia e Melo" |url=https://sicnoticias.pt/programas/expressodameianoite/2024-11-30-video-presidenciais-2026-se-o-pedro-passos-coelho-nao-for-eu-sou-capaz-de-ganhar-ao-almirante-gouveia-e-melo-11f511d3 |access-date=22 November 2025 |website=SIC Notícias |language=pt |archive-date=16 April 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250416184427/https://sicnoticias.pt/programas/expressodameianoite/2024-11-30-video-presidenciais-2026-se-o-pedro-passos-coelho-nao-for-eu-sou-capaz-de-ganhar-ao-almirante-gouveia-e-melo-11f511d3 |url-status=live }}</ref> as well as former party leaders [[Luís Marques Mendes]] and [[Rui Rio]], and former Minister [[Leonor Beleza]].<ref name=":13" /> Among these, Marques Mendes soon emerged as the leading candidate, announcing his candidacy in February 2025<ref name=":8" /> and receiving the support of the party in May 2025.<ref name=":14" /> Meanwhile, the [[CDS – People's Party|CDS–PP]], the junior partner of PSD in the [[Democratic Alliance (Portugal, 2024)|AD coalition]], hoped that the former leader and former deputy prime minister, [[Paulo Portas]], would run<ref>{{Cite web |last=Almeida |first=São José |date=11 February 2025 |title=CDS distante de Marques Mendes, continua à espera de Paulo Portas |url=https://www.publico.pt/2025/02/11/politica/noticia/cds-distante-marques-mendes-continua-espera-paulo-portas-2121992 |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=PÚBLICO |language=pt |archive-date=24 August 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250824190727/https://www.publico.pt/2025/02/11/politica/noticia/cds-distante-marques-mendes-continua-espera-paulo-portas-2121992 |url-status=live }}</ref> but, after he declined to seek the presidency,<ref>{{Cite web |last=Cunha |first=Mariana Lima |title=Sem Portas, CDS inclina-se para apoio pragmático a Marques Mendes |url=https://observador.pt/especiais/sem-portas-cds-inclina-se-para-apoio-pragmatico-a-marques-mendes/ |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=Observador |language=pt-PT |archive-date=2 November 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20251102090943/https://observador.pt/especiais/sem-portas-cds-inclina-se-para-apoio-pragmatico-a-marques-mendes/ |url-status=live }}</ref> CDS declared support for Marques Mendes, despite internal divisions.<ref name=":15" />

On the centre-left, [[Pedro Nuno Santos]] announced in January 2024, after being elected as [[Secretary-General of the Socialist Party (Portugal)|Secretary-general of the Socialist Party]], that he wanted the party to support a presidential candidate in the 2026 election,<ref>{{Cite web |last=Ralha |first=Leonardo |date=6 January 2024 |title=Pedro Nuno Santos garante que PS terá candidato presidencial em 2026 |url=https://www.dn.pt/politica/pedro-nuno-santos-garante-que-ps-tera-candidato-presidencial-em-2026 |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=Diário de Notícias |language=en}}</ref> as the last time the PS had supported a candidate was [[Manuel Alegre]] in the [[2011 Portuguese presidential election|2011 presidential election]]. Multiple socialist figures soon emerged as potential contenders, but, as the political landscape changed, many of these started losing momentum. Former [[List of presidents of the Assembly of the Republic (Portugal)|president of the Assembly of the Republic]], [[Augusto Santos Silva]], was considered as a strong candidate<ref name=":16" /> until he lost his seat as an MP in the [[2024 Portuguese legislative election|March 2024 legislative election]].<ref>{{Cite web |date=21 March 2024 |title=Santos Silva reconhece "derrota política e pessoal" e "abranda" nas presidenciais |url=https://www.publico.pt/2024/03/21/politica/noticia/augusto-santos-silva-reconhece-derrota-politica-pessoal-2084359 |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=PÚBLICO |language=pt |agency=Lusa}}</ref> The governor of the [[Banco de Portugal|Bank of Portugal]], [[Mário Centeno]], was also considered as a strong contender,<ref>{{Cite web |date=10 October 2024 |title=Centeno e Gouveia e Melo: como eles alimentam dois tabus presidenciais à espera do Governo |url=https://expresso.pt/politica/2024-10-10-centeno-e-gouveia-e-melo-como-eles-alimentam-dois-tabus-presidenciais-a-espera-do-governo-7743f619 |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=Expresso |language=pt}}</ref> due to his popularity as [[Ministry of Finance (Portugal)|Minister of Finance]], until he declined to run in January 2025.<ref>{{Cite web |date=15 January 2025 |title=Mário Centeno fora da corrida a Belém: "Não vou ser candidato a Presidente da República" |url=https://sicnoticias.pt/pais/2025-01-15-mario-centeno-fora-da-corrida-a-belem-nao-vou-ser-candidato-a-presidente-da-republica-ada48fc5 |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=SIC Notícias |language=pt |archive-date=19 January 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250119061718/https://sicnoticias.pt/pais/2025-01-15-mario-centeno-fora-da-corrida-a-belem-nao-vou-ser-candidato-a-presidente-da-republica-ada48fc5 |url-status=live }}</ref>

[[File:António José Seguro formaliza candidatura com entrega de 10.000 assinaturas.jpg|thumb|António José Seguro after formalizing his candidacy at the [[Constitutional Court (Portugal)|Constitutional Court]], 17 December 2025.]]
By then, two main candidates emerged to represent the traditional left, the former PS leader, [[António José Seguro]], who had been out of politics since 2014, and the former director-general of the [[International Organization for Migration]], [[António Vitorino]], as a group of high-profile socialists rejected the possibility of Seguro being the party's candidate,<ref>{{Cite web |last=Begonha |first=Ana Bacelar |date=18 January 2025 |title=Santos Silva: Vitorino "cumpre todos os requisitos", Seguro não cumpre "mínimos" |url=https://www.publico.pt/2025/01/18/politica/noticia/santos-silva-vitorino-cumpre-requisitos-seguro-nao-cumpre-minimos-2119287 |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=PÚBLICO |language=pt |archive-date=12 January 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260112204839/https://www.publico.pt/2025/01/18/politica/noticia/santos-silva-vitorino-cumpre-requisitos-seguro-nao-cumpre-minimos-2119287 |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=1 February 2025 |title=João Soares declara apoio a Seguro, que considera ser alvo de bullying no PS |url=https://www.publico.pt/2025/02/01/politica/noticia/joao-soares-declara-apoio-seguro-considera-alvo-bullying-ps-2121007 |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=PÚBLICO |language=pt |archive-date=12 June 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250612144118/https://www.publico.pt/2025/02/01/politica/noticia/joao-soares-declara-apoio-seguro-considera-alvo-bullying-ps-2121007 |url-status=live }}</ref> even suggesting an internal referendum to decide the party's presidential candidate,<ref>{{Cite web |date=9 January 2025 |title=Presidenciais: Ferro Rodrigues desafia Pedro Nuno a fazer primárias no PS para escolher candidato |url=https://expresso.pt/politica/eleicoes/presidenciais-2026/2025-01-09-presidenciais-ferro-rodrigues-desafia-pedro-nuno-a-fazer-primarias-no-ps-para-escolher-candidato-9daabe3c |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=Expresso |language=pt}}</ref> an idea that was ultimately rejected. Vitorino, who had by then failed to gain much support within the party's leadership as was expected,<ref>{{Cite web |date=10 February 2025 |title=Presidenciais: Nem uma voz se ouviu a defender Vitorino na Comissão Nacional do PS |url=https://expresso.pt/politica/2025-02-10-presidenciais-nem-uma-voz-se-ouviu-a-defender-vitorino-na-comissao-nacional-do-ps-27076430 |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=Expresso |language=pt |archive-date=15 February 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250215181837/https://expresso.pt/politica/2025-02-10-presidenciais-nem-uma-voz-se-ouviu-a-defender-vitorino-na-comissao-nacional-do-ps-27076430 |url-status=live }}</ref> ended up backing away from the race following the poor results of the PS in the [[2025 Portuguese legislative election|May 2025 legislative election]],<ref name=":17" /> paving the way for Seguro as the clear favorite to receive the party's support in the presidential election.<ref>{{Cite web |date=2 June 2025 |title=Seguro vai ser candidato à Presidência da República. Vitorino fora de cena |url=https://zap.aeiou.pt/seguro-candidato-belem-681692 |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=ZAP Notícias |language=pt-PT |archive-date=2 June 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250602092715/https://zap.aeiou.pt/seguro-candidato-belem-681692 |url-status=live }}</ref> Despite the attempt from Seguro's opponents to draft the runner-up of the [[2016 Portuguese presidential election|2016 presidential election]], [[António Sampaio da Nóvoa]],<ref>{{Cite web |date=14 August 2025 |title=Sem Sampaio da Nóvoa, críticos de Seguro atiram "toalha ao chão" |url=https://expresso.pt/politica/2025-08-14-sem-sampaio-da-novoa-criticos-de-seguro-atiram-toalha-ao-chao-d99873b8 |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=Expresso |language=pt}}</ref> and a final failed effort from Santos Silva to gain support to run,<ref name=":18" /> António José Seguro ended up announcing his candidacy in June 2025,<ref name=":19">{{Cite web |date=16 June 2025 |title=Presidenciais 2026. Seguro apresenta candidatura a Belém que "não é partidária nem nunca será" |url=https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/politica/presidenciais-2026-seguro-apresenta-candidatura-a-belem-que-nao-e-partidaria-nem-nunca-sera_n1662252 |access-date=19 August 2025 |website=RTP |language=pt |archive-date=16 July 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250716205922/https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/politica/presidenciais-2026-seguro-apresenta-candidatura-a-belem-que-nao-e-partidaria-nem-nunca-sera_n1662252 |url-status=live }}</ref> receiving an almost unanimous support of the PS following the [[2025 Portuguese local elections]].<ref name=":20">{{Cite web |date=19 October 2025 |title=Apoio a Seguro aprovado pela Comissão Nacional do PS |url=https://www.cmjornal.pt/politica/detalhe/apoio-a-seguro-aprovado-pela-comissao-nacional-do-ps |access-date=19 October 2025 |website=Correio da Manhã}}</ref>

On the left of the PS, the absence of Sampaio da Nóvoa from the race eliminated the prospects for a united left front,<ref>{{Cite web |date=6 August 2025 |title=Esquerda desespera com indecisão de Sampaio da Nóvoa: candidaturas próprias ganham terreno |url=https://expresso.pt/politica/eleicoes/presidenciais-2026/2025-08-06-esquerda-desespera-com-indecisao-de-sampaio-da-novoa-candidaturas-proprias-ganham-terreno-a6f2eb30 |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=Expresso |language=pt}}</ref> prompting each party to run their own candidates, despite attempts from Seguro supporters for a single left-wing candidacy.<ref>{{Cite web |author1=Rui Pedro Antunes |author2=Rita Tavares |title=Ana Gomes: "Candidatos à esquerda devem desistir antes da primeira volta a favor de Seguro" |url=https://observador.pt/especiais/ana-gomes-candidatos-a-esquerda-devem-desistir-antes-da-primeira-volta-a-favor-de-seguro/ |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=Observador |language=pt-PT |archive-date=24 November 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20251124171916/https://observador.pt/especiais/ana-gomes-candidatos-a-esquerda-devem-desistir-antes-da-primeira-volta-a-favor-de-seguro/ |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Frias |first=Rui |date=2 October 2025 |title=Presidenciais. Vasco Lourenço apoia Seguro e pede a António Filipe e Catarina Martins que desistam |url=https://www.dn.pt/pol%C3%ADtica/presidenciais-vasco-loureno-apoia-seguro-e-pede-a-antnio-filipe-e-catarina-martins-que-desistam |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=Diário de Notícias |language=en |archive-date=18 October 2025 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=24 October 2025 |title=Eurico Brilhante Dias desafia esquerda a apoiar Seguro – Renascença |url=https://rr.pt/artigo/hora-da-verdade/2025/10/24/eurico-brilhante-dias-desafia-esquerda-a-apoiar-seguro/445009/ |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=Rádio Renascença |language=pt-pt |url-status=live }}</ref> The [[Portuguese Communist Party|Communist Party]] announced the candidacy of former vice president of the [[Assembly of the Republic (Portugal)|Assembly of the Republic]], [[António Filipe (politician)|António Filipe]], in June 2025,<ref name="PCP">{{Cite web |date=29 June 2025 |title=António Filipe é o candidato presidencial apoiado pelo PCP |url=https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/politica/antonio-filipe-e-o-candidato-presidencial-apoiado-pelo-pcp_n1665704 |access-date=29 June 2025 |website=RTP |language=pt |archive-date=29 June 2025 resolutely stating that he would not withdraw from the race.<ref>{{Cite web |date=15 October 2025 |title=António Filipe recusa desistir de corrida a Belém a favor de outra candidatura |url=https://www.publico.pt/2025/10/15/politica/noticia/antonio-filipe-recusa-desistir-corrida-belem-favor-candidatura-2150921 |access-date=21 November 2025 |website=PÚBLICO |language=pt |agency=Lusa |archive-date=21 December 2025 }}</ref> [[Catarina Martins]], former leader of the [[Left Bloc]], and incumbent [[Member of the European Parliament|MEP]], entered the race in September 2025,<ref name=":21">{{Cite web |url-status=live }}</ref> followed in October by [[Jorge Pinto]], a [[LIVRE]] MP from [[Porto (Assembly of the Republic constituency)|Porto]].<ref name=":22">{{Cite web |last=Borges |first=Liliana |date=21 October 2025 |title=Jorge Pinto, do Livre, será candidato a Belém para "defender os ideais da República" |url=https://www.publico.pt/2025/10/22/politica/noticia/jorge-pinto-livre-sera-candidato-belem-defender-ideais-republica-2151691 |access-date=21 October 2025 |website=PÚBLICO |language=pt }}</ref> [[André Ventura]], the leader of the [[Radical right (Europe)|radical right-wing]] populist [[Chega]], and a candidate in the [[2021 Portuguese presidential election|previous presidential election]], announced his candidacy in January 2025.<ref name=":23">{{Cite web |author1=Inês André Figueiredo |author2=Cátia Rocha |date=4 January 2025 |title=André Ventura anuncia candidatura à For the liberals, with [[Rui Rocha]]'s re-election as leader of the [[Liberal Initiative]], parliamentary leader [[Mariana Leitão]] was initially designated as the party's presidential candidate.<ref presidential race.<ref name=":25">{{Cite web |author1=Joana Mesquita |author2=Maria Lopes |date=5 June 2025 |title=IL une-se em torno de Mariana Leitão, que abdica de candidatura presidencial |url-status=live }}</ref> In August, former leader and incumbent liberal MEP, [[João Cotrim de Figueiredo]], announced his candidacy,<ref name=":26">{{Cite web |last=Figueiredo |first=Inês André |title=Agora é oficial: João Cotrim Figueiredo é candidato à Presidência da República }}</ref> becoming a major right-wing contender, precluding figures, like outgoing mayor of Porto [[Rui Moreira]], from entering the race.<ref>{{Cite web |date=3 November 2025 |title=Rui Moreira admite que avanço de Cotrim o levou a não ser candidato a Belém |url=https://sicnoticias.pt/especiais/eleicoes-presidenciais/2025-11-03-video-rui-moreira-admite-que-avanco-de-cotrim-o-levou-a-nao-ser-candidato-a-belem-a4df44a8 |access-date=21 November 2025 ==Electoral system==
{{CSS image crop
| Image    = Voto antecipado presidenciais 2026.jpg
| bSize    = 250
| cWidth   = 250
| cHeight  = 170
| oTop     = 20
| oLeft    = 0
| Location = right
| Description = Early voting proving stamp for the 2026 presidential election.
}}

To stand for election, candidates must be of Portuguese origin and over 35 years old, gather 7,500 signatures of support one month before the election, and submit them to the [[Constitutional Court of Portugal]]. Then, the Constitutional Court has to certify if the candidacies submitted meet the requirements to appear on the ballot.<ref name="lexionario">{{Cite web |title=Presidente da República |url=https://diariodarepublica.pt/dr/lexionario/termo/presidente-republica |access-date=18 December 2024 |publisher=[[Diário da República]] |archive-date=27 February 2024 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240227195914/https://diariodarepublica.pt/dr/lexionario/termo/presidente-republica |url-status=live }}</ref> A candidate must receive a majority of votes (50% plus one vote) to be elected. If no candidate achieves a majority in the first round, a [[runoff election]] (i.e., second round, held between the two candidates who receive the most votes in the first round) has to be held.<ref name="lexionario" /> The highest number of candidacies ever accepted was ten, in 2016. Since the [[Carnation Revolution]], there had only been a single runoff election, in the [[1986 Portuguese presidential election]], when [[Diogo Freitas do Amaral]] (46.3% of votes in the first round and 48.8% in the second) lost to [[Mário Soares]] (25.4% in the first round and 51.2% in the second).

===Early voting===
Voters were also able to [[vote early]], with voting starting on 5 January for hospitalized and incarcerated voters and on 6 January for Portuguese citizens living abroad, ending for both groups on 8 January..<ref>{{Cite web |date=5 January 2026 |title=Voto antecipado: Saiba as regras, prazos e locais |url=https://novumcanal.pt/2026/01/voto-antecipado-saiba-as-regras-prazos-e-locais/ |access-date=11 January 2026 |website=NOVUM CANAL |language=pt-PT}}</ref> For citizens living in Portugal early voting occurred on 11 January, one week before election day, with voters having to register between 4 and 8 January to be eligible to cast an early ballot.<ref>{{Cite web |date=9 January 2026 |title=Presidenciais. Quer inscrever-se no voto antecipado? Saiba aqui como o pode fazer |url=https://eco.sapo.pt/2026/01/06/%F0%9F%93%B9-quer-inscrever-se-no-voto-antecipado-saiba-aqui-como-o-pode-fazer/ |access-date=6 January 2026 |website=eco.sapo.pt |language=pt-PT |archive-date=10 January 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260110024050/https://eco.sapo.pt/2026/01/06/%f0%9f%93%b9-quer-inscrever-se-no-voto-antecipado-saiba-aqui-como-o-pode-fazer/ |url-status=live }}</ref> By 8 January deadline, 218,481 voters (around 2% of the total of voters) had requested to vote early, a number lower than that recorded in 2021, during the [[COVID-19 pandemic in Portugal|COVID-19 pandemic]].<ref>{{Cite web |date=9 January 2026 |title=Mais de 218 mil eleitores inscritos para voto antecipado em mobilidade |url=https://www.publico.pt/2026/01/09/politica/noticia/218-mil-eleitores-inscritos-voto-antecipado-mobilidade-2160644 |access-date=9 January 2026 |website=Público |language=pt-PT |archive-date=9 January 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260109163758/https://www.publico.pt/2026/01/09/politica/noticia/218-mil-eleitores-inscritos-voto-antecipado-mobilidade-2160644 |url-status=live }}</ref> For the second round, 308,501 voters (around 3% of the total of voters) requested to cast an early ballot on 1 February, a considerably larger number than in the first round, with registration having been open between 24 and 29 January.<ref>{{Cite web |date=31 January 2026 |title=Cerca de 309 mil inscritos no voto antecipado, mais 90 mil do que na 1.ª volta |url=https://www.dnoticias.pt/2026/1/31/479692-cerca-de-309-mil-inscritos-no-voto-antecipado-mais-90-mil-do-que-na-1a-volta/ |access-date=30 January 2026 |website=Diário de Notícias da Madeira |language=pt-PT |archive-date=31 January 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260131222443/https://www.dnoticias.pt/2026/1/31/479692-cerca-de-309-mil-inscritos-no-voto-antecipado-mais-90-mil-do-que-na-1a-volta/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

==Candidates==

===Official candidates===
Candidates who formalized their candidacy and submitted enough signatures to the [[Constitutional Court (Portugal)|Constitutional Court]] that were accepted. The deadline to submit candidacies to the Court was 18 December 2025.<ref>{{Cite web |last=R. Malhado |first=Alexandre |date=9 December 2025 |title=Presidenciais: O sprint final das assinaturas |url=https://www.sabado.pt/portugal/detalhe/presidenciais-o-sprint-final-das-assinaturas |access-date=13 December 2025 |website=Sábado |language=pt |archive-date=10 December 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20251210020538/https://www.sabado.pt/portugal/detalhe/presidenciais-o-sprint-final-das-assinaturas |url-status=live }}</ref> Candidates are ordered by how they appeared on the ballot paper.<ref name=":candidatos">{{Cite web |last=R. Malhado |first=Alexandre |date=19 December 2025 |title=Gouveia e Melo vai em último nos boletins de voto que batem recorde de 14 candidatos |url=https://expresso.pt/politica/eleicoes/presidenciais-2026/2025-12-19-gouveia-e-melo-vai-em-ultimo-nos-boletins-de-voto-que-batem-recorde-de-14-candidatos-d79f545c |access-date=19 December 2025 |website=Expresso |language=en |archive-date=4 January 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260104231934/https://expresso.pt/politica/eleicoes/presidenciais-2026/2025-12-19-gouveia-e-melo-vai-em-ultimo-nos-boletins-de-voto-que-batem-recorde-de-14-candidatos-d79f545c |url-status=live }}</ref>

==Campaign==

===Issues===
The first round campaign was marked by issues like the state of [[healthcare in Portugal]]; [[Immigration to Portugal|immigration]]; the proposed changes to labour laws;<ref>{{cite web |date=13 November 2025 |title=Saúde e alterações laborais marcam a pré-campanha para as presidenciais |url=https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/politica/saude-e-alteracoes-laborais-marcam-a-pre-campanha-para-as-presidenciais_v1697976 |access-date=27 December 2025 |website=RTP |archive-date=13 December 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20251213103136/https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/politica/saude-e-alteracoes-laborais-marcam-a-pre-campanha-para-as-presidenciais_v1697976 |url-status=live }}</ref> the use of presidential powers, such as the use of the power to dissolve parliament; constitutional reform; [[foreign policy of Portugal|foreign policy]], being highlighted the [[Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)|war in Ukraine]], the presence of Portugal in [[NATO]], the involvement of [[Donald Trump]] in European affairs and the [[2026 United States strikes in Venezuela|situation in Venezuela]];<ref>{{Cite web |date=3 December 2025 |title=Debate das Presidenciais. António Filipe e Henrique Gouveia e Melo trocam acusações sobre situação de paz na Ucrânia |url-status=live }}</ref>

On 23 December 2025, a [[Judiciary of Portugal|court]] in [[Lisbon]] ordered [[Chega]] presidential candidate [[André Ventura]] to remove campaign posters [[Negative campaigning|targeting]] the [[Romani people in Portugal|Romani people]] within 24 hours, ruling that the materials are [[Racism in Portugal|discriminatory]] and may [[Incitement to ethnic or racial hatred|incite hatred]], or face daily fines of €2,500 (US$2,940) per poster.<ref>{{cite web |date=26 December 2025 |title=Portugal's far-right leader ordered to remove posters targeting Roma community |url=https://www.reuters.com/world/portugals-far-right-leader-ordered-remove-posters-targeting-roma-community-2025-12-22/ |work=Reuters}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |date=26 December 2025 |title=Portugal's far-right Chega party ordered to take down posters targeting Roma people |url=https://www.theguardian.com/world/2025/dec/22/portugals-far-right-chega-party-ordered-to-take-down-posters-targeting-roma-people |work=The Guardian}}</ref> Also, the last few days of the first round campaign were dominated by an accusation of sexual harassment against [[João Cotrim de Figueiredo]] by a former female IL parliamentary aid, with the candidate denying the accusation and even filing a lawsuit against the woman who accused him, despite new revelations and contradictions being reported.<ref>{{cite web |date=15 January 2026 |title=Acusação de assédio na IL. "Ela disse-me quem a tinha assediado e que toda a gente sabia no grupo parlamentar" |url=https://www.dn.pt/sociedade/acusao-de-assdio-na-il-ela-disse-me-quem-a-tinha-assediado-e-que-toda-a-gente-sabia-no-grupo-parlamentar |access-date=15 January 2026 |work=Diário de Notícias |archive-date=15 January 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260115183816/https://www.dn.pt/sociedade/acusao-de-assdio-na-il-ela-disse-me-quem-a-tinha-assediado-e-que-toda-a-gente-sabia-no-grupo-parlamentar |url-status=live }}</ref>

The campaign for the second round was limited, as bad weather across the country, and the devastating impact of [[2025–26 European windstorm season#Storm Kristin|Storm Kristin]], after 28 January, all but put a halt to the campaign,<ref>{{cite web |date=30 January 2026 |title=Segunda volta arrancou a meio gás: resta uma semana de campanha |url=https://expresso.pt/politica/eleicoes/presidenciais-2026/2026-01-29-segunda-volta-arrancou-a-meio-gas-resta-uma-semana-de-campanha-d016123a |access-date=30 January 2026 |work=Expresso |archive-date=30 January 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260130112130/https://expresso.pt/politica/eleicoes/presidenciais-2026/2026-01-29-segunda-volta-arrancou-a-meio-gas-resta-uma-semana-de-campanha-d016123a |url-status=live }}</ref> despite the discussion about the "wave" of center-right endorsements for Seguro and the government's response to the storm aftermath being dominant.<ref>{{cite web |date=30 January 2026 |title=Seguro continua a somar apoios. Candidatos rumam ao distrito de Aveiro no terceiro dia de campanha |url=https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/politica/seguro-continua-a-somar-apoios-candidatos-rumam-ao-distrito-de-aveiro-no-terceiro-dia-de-campanha_n1714305 |access-date=30 January 2026 |work=RTP |archive-date=30 January 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260130092518/https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/politica/seguro-continua-a-somar-apoios-candidatos-rumam-ao-distrito-de-aveiro-no-terceiro-dia-de-campanha_n1714305 |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{cite web |date=5 February 2026 |title=Mau tempo troca as voltas a Ventura com mira quase sempre apontada ao Governo |url=https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/politica/mau-tempo-troca-as-voltas-a-ventura-com-mira-quase-sempre-apontada-ao-governo_n1716166 |access-date=6 February 2026 |work=RTP |archive-date=5 February 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260205073908/https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/politica/mau-tempo-troca-as-voltas-a-ventura-com-mira-quase-sempre-apontada-ao-governo_n1716166 |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{cite web |date=5 February 2026 |title="Fico chocado com a deficiência do Estado na resposta às populações", critica Seguro |url=https://sicnoticias.pt/especiais/eleicoes-presidenciais/2026/2026-02-05-video-fico-chocado-com-a-deficiencia-do-estado-na-resposta-as-populacoes-critica-seguro-0ddac5c9 |access-date=6 February 2026 |work=SIC Notícias |archive-date=6 February 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260206192610/https://sicnoticias.pt/especiais/eleicoes-presidenciais/2026/2026-02-05-video-fico-chocado-com-a-deficiencia-do-estado-na-resposta-as-populacoes-critica-seguro-0ddac5c9 |url-status=live }}</ref>

===Candidates' debates===

====First round====
The three main TV channels in Portugal, [[RTP1]], [[Sociedade Independente de Comunicação|SIC]] and [[Televisão Independente|TVI]], agreed to host 28 one-on-one debates between the 8 main candidates on the ballot for the first round.<ref>{{Cite web |date=28 October 2025 |title=RTP, SIC e TVI garantem que todos os candidatos presidenciais aceitaram modelo de debates e rejeitam "exclusividade" |url=https://www.dn.pt/pol%C3%ADtica/rtp-sic-e-tvi-garantem-que-todos-os-candidatos-presidenciais-aceitaram-modelo-de-debates-e-rejeitam-exclusividade |access-date=29 October 2025 |website=Diário de Notícias |language=pt-pt |archive-date=29 October 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20251029170536/https://www.dn.pt/pol%C3%ADtica/rtp-sic-e-tvi-garantem-que-todos-os-candidatos-presidenciais-aceitaram-modelo-de-debates-e-rejeitam-exclusividade |url-status=live }}</ref> However, this format was challenged, as the three main broadcasters were accused of forcing an exclusivity agreement so that the debates only take place on these three channels.<ref>{{Cite web |date=28 October 2025 |title=RTP, SIC e TVI exigem exclusividade aos candidatos presidenciais para participarem nos debates televisivos |url=https://www.dn.pt/pol%C3%ADtica/rtp-sic-e-tvi-exigem-exclusividade-aos-candidatos-presidenciais-para-participarem-nos-debates-televisivos |access-date=29 October 2025 |website=Diário de Notícias |language=pt-pt |archive-date=28 October 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20251028130722/https://www.dn.pt/pol%C3%ADtica/rtp-sic-e-tvi-exigem-exclusividade-aos-candidatos-presidenciais-para-participarem-nos-debates-televisivos |url-status=live }}</ref> [[CMTV]] filed a complaint to the {{ill|National Electoral Commission (Portugal)|pt|Comissão Nacional de Eleições|lt=Electoral Commission}} (CNE) against RTP, SIC and TVI, accusing the networks of an "anti-competitive matrix".<ref>{{Cite web |date=29 October 2025 |title=Dona da CMTV apresenta queixa da RTP, SIC e TVI devido a debates presidenciais |url=https://sapo.pt/artigo/dona-da-cmtv-apresenta-queixa-da-rtp-sic-e-tvi-devido-a-debates-presidenciais-69029d651ce2e9935fc2b8de |access-date=29 October 2025 |work=Espresso |language=pt-pt |via=Sapo}}</ref> The channels denied such agreement, but the media regulator ruled in favour of CMTV and advised for the debate format to include the [[plaintiff]].<ref>{{Cite web |date=14 November 2025 |title=ERC dá razão à Medialivre e recomenda alargamento do acordo para transmissão dos debates |url=https://eco.sapo.pt/2025/11/14/erc-da-razao-a-medialivre-e-recomenda-alargamento-do-acordo-para-transmissao-dos-debates-das-eleicoes-presidenciais/ |access-date=16 November 2025 |website=Eco |language=pt-pt |archive-date=17 December 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20251217043142/https://eco.sapo.pt/2025/11/14/erc-da-razao-a-medialivre-e-recomenda-alargamento-do-acordo-para-transmissao-dos-debates-das-eleicoes-presidenciais/ |url-status=live }}</ref> Despite this advise, the 3 channels decided to not change the format.<ref>{{Cite web |date=14 November 2025 |title=Debates vão decorrer tal como previsto nos três canais generalistas |url=https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/eleicoes-presidenciais-2026/debates-vao-decorrer-tal-como-previsto-nos-tres-canais-generalistas_n1698286 |access-date=16 November 2025 |website=RTP Notícias |language=pt-pt |archive-date=14 November 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20251114173917/https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/eleicoes-presidenciais-2026/debates-vao-decorrer-tal-como-previsto-nos-tres-canais-generalistas_n1698286 |url-status=live }}</ref> [[List of radio stations in Portugal|Radio stations]] also hosted a debate with the 8 main candidates,<ref name="radio">{{Cite web |date=16 December 2025 |title=Candidatos presidenciais enfrentam-se no Debate da Rádio a 2 de janeiro |url=https://observador.pt/2025/12/15/candidatos-presidenciais-enfrentam-se-no-debate-da-radio-a-2-de-janeiro/ |access-date=16 December 2025 |website=Observador |language=pt-pt |archive-date=18 December 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20251218055916/https://observador.pt/2025/12/15/candidatos-presidenciais-enfrentam-se-no-debate-da-radio-a-2-de-janeiro/ |url-status=live }}</ref> while [[RTP1]] hosted a debate with all 11 candidates on the ballot.<ref name="tv">{{Cite web |date=26 December 2025 |title=Todos contra todos: RTP junta os 11 candidatos no Dia de Reis para o último debate presidencial |url=https://expresso.pt/politica/eleicoes/presidenciais-2026/debates-presidenciais/2025-12-26-todos-contra-todos-rtp-junta-os-11-candidatos-no-dia-de-reis-para-o-ultimo-debate-presidencial-b0159912 |access-date=26 December 2025 |website=Expresso |language=pt-PT |archive-date=26 December 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20251226133841/https://expresso.pt/politica/eleicoes/presidenciais-2026/debates-presidenciais/2025-12-26-todos-contra-todos-rtp-junta-os-11-candidatos-no-dia-de-reis-para-o-ultimo-debate-presidencial-b0159912 |url-status=live }}</ref>

====Second round====
The three main TV channels, RTP1, SIC and TVI, agreed to invite the two candidates in the second round for a debate, with both Seguro and Ventura accepting the invitation. The debate was held on 27 January and broadcast simultaneously by the three major networks.<ref name=":debate2">{{Cite web |date=22 January 2026 |title=Debate entre Seguro e Ventura marcado para terça-feira |url=https://expresso.pt/politica/eleicoes/presidenciais-2026/2026-01-22-debate-entre-seguro-e-ventura-marcado-para-terca-feira-d356adcd |access-date=22 January 2026 |website=Expresso |language=pt-pt}}</ref> Radio stations also invited the two candidates for a one-on-one debate, but the Seguro campaign rejected this invitation and a debate was not held.<ref>{{Cite web |date=23 January 2026 |title=Seguro não aceita participar no debate das rádios, que não se realiza na segunda volta |url=https://observador.pt/2026/01/23/seguro-nao-aceita-participar-no-debate-das-radios-que-nao-se-realiza-na-segunda-volta/ |access-date=23 January 2026 |website=Observador |language=pt-pt}}</ref>

==Voter turnout==
The table below shows voter turnout throughout election day including voters from Overseas.

===First round===
{{Table alignment}}

| style="text-align: center;" colspan="10" | Sources<ref name="afluencia">{{cite web |title=Presidenciais 2026 – Afluência |url=https://www.eleicoes.mai.gov.pt/presidenciais2026_1S/afluencia |access-date=5 February 2026 |website=Eleicoes |publisher=[[Ministry of Internal Administration (Portugal)|Ministry of Internal Administration]] |language=pt}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |title=Presidenciais 2021 – Afluência |url=https://www.eleicoes.mai.gov.pt/presidenciais2021/afluencia |access-date=9 June 2023 |website=Eleicoes |publisher=[[Ministry of Internal Administration (Portugal)|Ministry of Internal Administration]] |language=pt}}</ref>
|}

Voter turnout was higher in the first round compared to 2021, with 5,77 million voters casting a ballot, the second highest number of votes cast in a first round presidential election in Portuguese history, only surpassed by the [[1980 Portuguese presidential election|1980 turnout]], and the third highest turnout in a national election in 30 years, only behind the [[2024 Portuguese legislative election|2024]] and [[2025 Portuguese legislative election|2025 legislative elections]].<ref>{{Cite news |date=19 January 2026 |title=Presidenciais de 2026 com a segunda maior participação de sempre |url=https://rr.pt/especial/politica/2026/01/19/presidenciais-de-2026-com-a-segunda-maior-participacao-de-sempre/455847/ |access-date=23 January 2026 |work=Renascença |language=Portuguese}}</ref> In Portugal alone, the turnout rate stood at 61.50 percent, an increase of 16.05 percent compared with 2021,<ref>{{cite news |date=18 January 2026 |title=Presidenciais 2026 – Território Nacional |url=https://www.presidenciais2026.mai.gov.pt/resultados/territorio-nacional |access-date=23 January 2026 |publisher=mai.gov.pt |language=pt}}</ref> while in Overseas constituencies, [[Europe (Assembly of the Republic constituency)|Europe]] and [[Outside Europe (Assembly of the Republic constituency)|Outside of Europe]], the turnout rate also increased to 4.09%, compared with the 1.88% from 2021.<ref>{{Cite news |title=Desta vez foram 70 mil e quase 400 votantes na diáspora |url=https://www.instagram.com/p/DTzmiFtEb1R/ |access-date=1 June 2025 |work=rdpinternacionalrtp Instagram |language=Portuguese}}</ref> The overall share of voter turnout, Portugal alone and Overseas combined, stood at 52.39%, a 13.13 percent increase compared with 2021.<ref name=Oficiais />

Due to [[Storm Kristin]] and its resulting [[flood]]s, some localities postponed the second round voting to 15 February: the municipalities of [[Alcácer do Sal]], [[Arruda dos Vinhos]], [[Golegã]], and parts of the municipalities of [[Cartaxo]] (parish of Valada), [[Leiria]] (parish of Bidoeira de Cima), [[Rio Maior]] (parish of [[Alcobertas]] and part of parish of Rio Maior), [[Salvaterra de Magos]] (parish of [[Salvaterra de Magos e Foros de Salvaterra|Salvaterra de Magos]]), and [[Santarém, Portugal|Santarém]] (parts of the city of Santarém and of the parish of [[São Vicente do Paul e Vale de Figueira|São Vicente do Paul]]).<ref>{{cite web |date=5 February 2026 |title=As Câmaras que já anunciaram o adiamento das eleições Presidenciais |trans-title=The chambers that have already announced the postponement of the presidential elections |url=https://sicnoticias.pt/especiais/eleicoes-presidenciais/2026/2026-02-05-as-camaras-que-ja-anunciaram-o-adiamento-das-eleicoes-presidenciais-f28fadf3 |access-date=6 February 2026 |website=SIC Notícias |language=pt}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |date=6 February 2026 |title=Pombal diz ter condições para realizar eleições presidenciais no domingo |trans-title=Pombal says it has the conditions to hold presidential elections on Sunday |url=https://sicnoticias.pt/especiais/eleicoes-presidenciais/2026/2026-02-06-pombal-diz-ter-condicoes-para-realizar-eleicoes-presidenciais-no-domingo-a782313b |access-date=6 February 2026 |website=SIC Notícias |language=pt}}</ref> These localities comprised 36,852 registered voters.<ref name="postponed">{{Cite web |date=7 February 2026 |title=Segundo Sufrágio PR 2026, Adiamento da votação |trans-title=Second round of 2026 presidential election, postponement of voting |url=https://www.cne.pt/sites/default/files/dl/eleicoes/2026_pr/docs_geral/pr2026_2.sufragio_lista_adiamento_votacao-v4.pdf |publisher=Comissão Nacional de Eleições |language=pt}}</ref> Despite fears of low turnout,<ref>{{Cite web |date=26 January 2026 |title=Segunda volta das presidenciais arrisca agravar abstenção, mas rejeição a Ventura pode mobilizar eleitores |url=https://executivedigest.sapo.pt/segunda-volta-das-presidenciais-arrisca-agravar-abstencao-mas-rejeicao-a-ventura-pode-mobilizar-eleitores/ |publisher=Executive Digest |access-date=18 February 2026 |language=pt}}</ref> it was only slightly lower than in the first round, with 5,52 million voters casting a ballot, an overall voter turnout share of 50 percent.<ref>{{Cite web |date=15 February 2026 |title=Presidenciais. Abstenção foi de 50% na segunda volta |url=https://www.rtp.pt/noticias/politica/presidenciais-abstencao-foi-de-50-na-segunda-volta_n1719375 |publisher=RTP |access-date=18 February 2026 |language=pt}}</ref> In Portugal alone, the turnout rate dropped to 58.67 percent, while on the other hand, Overseas constituencies saw a turnout increase to 4.83 percent.<ref name="results2" />

==Results==
[[File:Discurso de vitória de António José Seguro na primeira volta das presidenciais.png|thumb|Seguro delivering his victory speech on 18 January 2026]]

Seguro was the clear winner of the first round, winning 18 districts, while Ventura won only Faro and Madeira;<ref>{{Cite news |date=18 January 2026 |title=Presidenciais 2026: distrito a distrito, quem ganha e com quantos votos |url=https://sapo.pt/artigo/presidenciais-2026-distrito-a-distrito-quem-vai-a-frente-e-com-quantos-votos-696d5b864feb524eb608b732 |access-date=22 January 2026 |website=Sapo}}</ref> however, voters overseas gave a clear victory to Ventura.<ref>{{Cite news |date=19 January 2026 |title=Do Canadá à Austrália: assim votaram os portugueses no estrangeiro |url=https://cnnportugal.iol.pt/eleicoes-presidenciais-2026/presidente-da-republica/do-canada-a-australia-assim-votaram-os-portugueses-no-estrangeiro/20260119/696d6753d34e92a34497a203 |access-date=22 January 2026 |website=CNN Portugal}}</ref> In terms of [[Municipalities of Portugal|municipalities]], Seguro won 225 out of the country's 308 municipalities, while Ventura was the winner in 80 of them, and Marques Mendes only won 3 municipalities.<ref>{{Cite news |date=19 January 2026 |title=Quem ganhou em cada concelho: Seguro ganhou 160 à AD e 30 ao Chega, Ventura ganhou em 80 e toda a Madeira, Mendes só ganhou em três |url=https://expresso.pt/politica/eleicoes/presidenciais-2026/2026-01-19-quem-ganhou-em-cada-concelho-seguro-ganhou-160-a-ad-e-30-ao-chega-ventura-ganhou-em-80-e-toda-a-madeira-mendes-so-ganhou-em-tres-a034beec |access-date=22 January 2026 |website=Expresso}}</ref>

In his first round victory speech, Seguro said that he is "free" and "lives without shackles", adding that there were "no losers" because "we are all democrats". Ventura criticized "socialism" during his election night speech, saying he would "lead the non-socialist space in Portugal" and that "socialism kills, socialism corrupts". João Cotrim Figueiredo criticized Prime Minister [[Luís Montenegro]], who announced his neutrality in the second round, saying that he was to blame for the country having a "President from the PS". Henrique Gouveia e Melo conceded defeat, saying the results "did not meet the objectives" he had set, and that there was the "need to depoliticize the Presidency of the Republic". Luís Marques Mendes also conceded defeat, assuming full responsibility for the outcome. Catarina Martins, António Filipe, and Jorge Pinto endorsed Seguro in their election night speeches.<ref name="discurso">{{Cite news |date=19 January 2026 |title=Do 'somos todos democratas' de Seguro ao 'socialismo corrompe' de Ventura. As frases da noite eleitoral |url=https://eco.sapo.pt/2026/01/19/do-somos-todos-democratas-de-seguro-ao-socialismo-corrompe-de-ventura-as-frases-da-noite-eleitoral/ |access-date=22 January 2026 |website=ECO}}</ref>

In the second round, Seguro achieved a landslide victory, winning 306 out of 308 municipalities, 3,115 out of 3,259 civil parishes and all 20 districts, however trailing Ventura by 1,366 in the overseas vote but winning 70 out of 107 consulates.<ref>{{Cite news |date=8 February 2026 |title=Quanto recordes bateu Seguro e quantos ficaram por bater? |url=https://rr.pt/noticia/politica/2026/02/08/quanto-recordes-bateu-seguro-e-quantos-ficaram-por-bater/458676/ |access-date=9 February 2026 |website=Renascença}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |date=9 February 2026 |title=Seguro vence todos os distritos de Portugal e Ventura só deve ganhar no estrangeiro. 13 curiosidades sobre as presidenciais |url=https://observador.pt/especiais/seguro-vence-todos-os-distritos-de-portugal-e-ventura-so-deve-ganhar-no-estrangeiro-13-curiosidades-sobre-as-presidenciais/ |access-date=9 February 2026 |website=Observador}}</ref> In his speech as President-elect, Seguro promised political stability and "loyalty and institutional cooperation" with the government, while being a "demanding President".<ref>{{Cite news |date=9 February 2026 |title='Não será por mim que a legislatura será interrompida'. Seguro garante 'cooperação' com o Governo |url=https://eco.sapo.pt/2026/02/09/nao-sera-por-mim-que-a-legislatura-sera-interrompida-seguro-garante-cooperacao-com-o-governo/ |access-date=9 February 2026 |website=ECO}}</ref> Ventura conceded defeat, but argued that the results were just a "path" to change and leading the government of Portugal, saying that the party was now the leading right-wing force in Portugal.<ref>{{Cite news |date=9 February 2026 |title='Mesmo não vencendo, tivemos o melhor resultado de sempre. Vamos governar', afirma Ventura |url=https://eco.sapo.pt/2026/02/09/mesmo-nao-vencendo-tivemos-o-melhor-resultado-de-sempre-vamos-governar-afirma-ventura/ |access-date=9 February 2026 |website=ECO}}</ref>
